# Decisions

Append-at-top. One row per decision; rows are IMMUTABLE — a changed decision gets a NEW row whose
what-cell opens "supersedes D-NNN:"; the old row is never edited. WHY ≤ 2 lines; the full rationale
lives at the WHERE links. Subagents and the pipeline never hold the pen — the dispatching session
appends. Query: grep this file first; fleet-wide: `python3 /opt/fabrik/scripts/decisions.py <term>`
(`--check` validates ledger integrity — its own `--help` is the current check inventory).

| D-210 | 2026-09-09 | fleet, answering fabrik-lib's direct question (mail 01M212G4C9K9E3W6WS9WJSVV35: "should this hook still exist? … Your call; the installer is yours") | **The fabrik-lib `post-commit` re-vendor of `subagents/` into the hub copy STAYS, until the hub decides the fate of `/opt/fabrik/libs/subagents` — and the hub copy's status is what that decision must settle FIRST.** fabrik-lib retired the module on 2026-09-07 (their D-132, operator ruling on cost), froze the canonical at `88b3b8d4`, and instructed 47 repos to delete their copies — but their `post-commit` hook still rsyncs canonical into `/opt/fabrik/libs/subagents` on every commit under `subagents/`, so their own retirement commits propagated into the hub without either side intending it. Their inclination was to disable it only after the hub settles its copy; I agree and adopt it. REASONING: the hub copy is still LOAD-BEARING (importers read it) and D-137 forbids the hub from modifying the vendored module, so disabling the sync now would freeze a copy the hub may not edit and fabrik-lib may no longer refresh — strictly worse than the present state. Instead: disjoint slices, one model each by what it is good at — Haiku the grep-shaped classes (stale phrases, table shape, fence parity, dead symbols) as one seat per class across the whole surface; Sonnet the ordinary code and doc units; Opus ONLY the risky units (concurrency, locks, the record format, anything fleet-synced); Fable orchestrates, adjudicates and executes the refutations. The union of the slices IS one full pass, every file read once — on this review, ~5 seats per round instead of 10. (4) All four models, each once, where they pay; after round 1 a model sweeps only if ITS slice changed, and most of Haiku's work should become a SCRIPT at close rather than a seat at all. (5) DELTA rounds: round 1 is the only full pass; every later round reviews the previous round's FIX DIFF plus its callers under the same partition rule, and the review closes when a delta round confirms zero.
